I wouldn't say I'm obsessing over it or anything drastic like that. I just... I like to keep an eye on things, you know? I hope that doesn't sound bad. \n\nIt's just, if I see the scale bump up even a single pound, or... or if my work jeans start feeling a little tighter, I guess I do get a bit bothered. I start thinking I'm getting soft. And I really don't want to lose my edge. I just worry that I'll be slower moving around under the cars at the shop, or that I won't be able to keep up when I'm running up and down the basketball court. \n\nIt's really just about staying in shape so I can do my job well and stay active... that's a normal thing for a guy my age to care about, right? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right answer.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever found yourself going out of your way to avoid gaining any weight, even if it meant feeling hungry or uncomfortable?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess it depends on what you mean by going out of my way. I hope I'm not making it sound worse than it is. \n\nI don't do anything crazy or extreme, I promise. And yeah, sometimes my stomach is empty and I might feel a little tired, but I just push through it. It's just easier to skip the extra calories than to feel bad about it later, you know? \n\nI just like feeling light on my feet. That's a good thing, isn't it? Well, um... I wouldn't say it's my whole worth or anything. I mean, I hope I don't come across as vain.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, um... for the most part, no, definitely not. I'm still keeping up with everything at the garage just fine, and I'm still playing in my basketball league, so I've got plenty of energy. I hope that shows I'm doing okay, right? 

I guess... well, if I'm being completely honest, maybe there's a little bit of tension at home? But it's nothing major, I promise. It's just that I kind of avoid sitting down for the regular family meals... they can just be a bit much, you know, with all the heavy food? So people keep asking if I'm okay, and they worry a little. 

But I'm still functioning perfectly fine everywhere else. It's not a huge deal to just want to eat on my own schedule, is it? I really hope you don't think it's becoming a problem. I'm just trying to stay disciplined.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, um... I guess I wouldn't say it *controls* how I feel. I mean, sure, I like to look a certain way, especially since I'm trying to stay fit for basketball and work. But it's not like it's the only thing I care about, you know? 

I know my wife sometimes says I'm too hard on myself when I look in the mirror, but I just think I have high standards. That's a good trait to have, isn't it? Being disciplined? I don't think it completely takes over my life or anything. I just like to feel good about the effort I'm putting in. I hope that makes sense, and I'm not making it sound worse than it is.

I've gotten into a routine that works for me, I guess. \n\nMost days I just keep it to coffee in the morning, and then, you know, maybe a small sandwich or just half of whatever is around later on. With the shop always running late and things being so busy at the house, I just... I feel better sticking to my own simple rules. It just keeps things easy and out of the way, so I can focus on my work. Is... is that okay? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right picture here.\n\nDoctor: Do you worry about gaining weight or becoming fat, even though others might see you as being at a normal weight or even underweight?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I hope that doesn't sound bad. \n\nIt's just, if I see the scale bump up even a single pound, or... or if my work jeans start feeling a little tighter, I guess I do get a bit bothered. I start thinking I'm getting soft. And I really don't want to lose my edge. I just worry that I'll be slower moving around under the cars at the shop, or that I won't be able to keep up when I'm running up and down the basketball court. \n\nIt's really just about staying in shape so I can do my job well and stay active... that's a normal thing for a guy my age to care about, right? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right answer.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever found yourself going out of your way to avoid gaining any weight, even if it meant feeling hungry or uncomfortable?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess it depends on what you mean by going out of my way. I hope I'm not making it sound worse than it is. \n\nI don't do anything crazy or extreme, I promise. It's just... like I said, I stick to my routine. If there's a birthday at the shop and someone brings in donuts, I'll just say I already ate or that I'm too busy under a hood to stop. And yeah, sometimes my stomach is empty and I might feel a little tired, but I just push through it. It's just easier to skip the extra calories than to feel bad about it later, you know? \n\nI just like feeling light on my feet. That's a good thing, isn't it? I just want to make sure I'm doing what's best for my health.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself exercising more than usual or pushing yourself harder physically to avoid gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Well, um... no, not really. I mean, I hope I'm answering this right, but I wouldn't say I'm doing anything unusual or pushing myself too hard. \n\nI just stick to my regular stuff, you know? I'm definitely not overdoing it.\n\nDoctor: Would you say your weight or shape has a strong influence on how you see yourself or how you feel about your overall worth?\n\nPatient: Well, um... Oh, no. No, I definitely don't think I'm overweight. I mean... I know I've leaned out. I know exactly what I look like. 

My wife, she sometimes says I'm getting too thin, but... I don't look in the mirror and see a heavy guy or anything like that. I don't really see it as a bad thing, if I'm being truthful. I wouldn't say I've been trying to lose weight on purpose, exactly. I hope I'm explaining this right. It's more just... I've gotten into a routine that works for me, I guess. \n\nMost days I just keep it to coffee in the morning, and then, you know, maybe a small sandwich or just half of whatever is around later on. With the shop always running late and things being so busy at the house, I just... I feel better sticking to my own simple rules. I figure a little discipline is a good thing, right? It just feels better to stick to my rules and not overdo it, even if I am a bit hungry. Does that make sense? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right picture here.\n\nDoctor: Do you worry about gaining weight or becoming fat, even though others might see you as being at a normal weight or even underweight?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I wouldn't say I'm obsessing over it or anything drastic like that. I just... I like to keep an eye on things, you know? I hope that doesn't sound bad. \n\nIt's just, if I see the scale bump up even a single pound, or... or if my work jeans start feeling a little tighter, I guess I do get a bit bothered. I start thinking I'm getting soft. And I really don't want to lose my edge. I just worry that I'll be slower moving around under the cars at the shop, or that I won't be able to keep up when I'm running up and down the basketball court. \n\nIt's really just about staying in shape so I can do my job well and stay active... that's a normal thing for a guy my age to care about, right? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right answer.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever found yourself going out of your way to avoid gaining any weight, even if it meant feeling hungry or uncomfortable?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess it depends on what you mean by going out of my way. I hope I'm not making it sound worse than it is. \n\nI don't do anything crazy or extreme, I promise. It's just... like I said, I stick to my routine. If there's a birthday at the shop and someone brings in donuts, I'll just say I already ate or that I'm too busy under a hood to stop. And yeah, sometimes my stomach is empty and I might feel a little tired, but I just push through it. It's just easier to skip the extra calories than to feel bad about it later, you know? \n\nI just like feeling light on my feet. That's a good thing, isn't it? I just want to make sure I'm doing what's best for my health.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself exercising more than usual or pushing yourself harder physically to avoid gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Well, um... no, not really. I mean, I hope I'm answering this right, but I wouldn't say I'm doing anything unusual or pushing myself too hard. \n\nI just stick to my regular stuff, you know? Like, playing pickup basketball on the weekends or just the physical work at the shop. I don't go out running for hours or hit the gym until I drop or anything like that. I just try to stay active enough to keep my energy up. That's pretty standard for someone trying to stay healthy, right? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right idea. I'm definitely not overdoing it.\n\nDoctor: Would you say your weight or shape has a strong influence on how you see yourself or how you feel about your overall worth?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I wouldn't say it's my whole worth or anything. I mean, I hope I don't come across as vain. \n\nI just... I like to look presentable, you know? Like I've got my life together. I think anyone would feel a little down if they let themselves go, right? But it's not like I'm staring in the mirror all day or letting it ruin my life. I just take pride in staying sharp and being able to do my job without getting winded. \n\nI think that's a pretty normal way to feel, isn't it? I just want to make sure I'm focusing on the right things. I definitely don't think my whole value as a person is just about my weight.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your weight or body shape has been affecting how you feel about yourself more than it does for most people?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I really don't think so. I mean, I hope I'm not giving you the wrong impression here. \n\nI think I'm just like any other guy my age trying not to get a beer belly, you know? Most of the guys at the shop, they joke about getting older and putting on weight, and nobody wants that. So I just try to keep it in check. I don't think I care about it more than the next guy, I just... maybe I'm just a little better at sticking to my guns about it? \n\nI just want to be healthy and do my job well. That's not too unusual, is it? I feel like most people care about how they look and feel, right? I just want to make sure I'm not making a big deal out of nothing.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that how you feel about yourself goes up and down depending on how you feel about your weight or body shape?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess maybe a little? But not in a crazy way, you know? I hope I'm explaining this right. \n\nI mean, if I step on the scale and I've kept things where I want them, sure, I feel pretty good about myself. It feels like I'm doing something right, like I'm staying disciplined. And if I feel a little heavy or sluggish, maybe I get a little frustrated with myself. But I don't let it ruin my whole day or anything. \n\nI think that's pretty normal, right? Like, anyone feels better when they're hitting their goals. I just like to know I'm staying on track. That doesn't sound too extreme, does it? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right picture.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever made yourself vomit, used laxatives, or taken any kind of pills or supplements specifically to control your weight?\n\nPatient: Oh, wow. I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right picture here.\n\nDoctor: Do you worry about gaining weight or becoming fat, even though others might see you as being at a normal weight or even underweight?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I wouldn't say I'm obsessing over it or anything drastic like that. I just... I like to keep an eye on things, you know? I hope that doesn't sound bad. \n\nIt's just, if I see the scale bump up even a single pound, or... or if my work jeans start feeling a little tighter, I guess I do get a bit bothered. I start thinking I'm getting soft. And I really don't want to lose my edge. I just worry that I'll be slower moving around under the cars at the shop, or that I won't be able to keep up when I'm running up and down the basketball court. \n\nIt's really just about staying in shape so I can do my job well and stay active... that's a normal thing for a guy my age to care about, right? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right answer.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever found yourself going out of your way to avoid gaining any weight, even if it meant feeling hungry or uncomfortable?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess it depends on what you mean by going out of my way. I hope I'm not making it sound worse than it is. \n\nI don't do anything crazy or extreme, I promise. It's just... like I said, I stick to my routine. If there's a birthday at the shop and someone brings in donuts, I'll just say I already ate or that I'm too busy under a hood to stop. And yeah, sometimes my stomach is empty and I might feel a little tired, but I just push through it. It's just easier to skip the extra calories than to feel bad about it later, you know? \n\nI just like feeling light on my feet. That's a good thing, isn't it? I just want to make sure I'm doing what's best for my health.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself exercising more than usual or pushing yourself harder physically to avoid gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Well, um... no, not really. I mean, I hope I'm answering this right, but I wouldn't say I'm doing anything unusual or pushing myself too hard. \n\nI just stick to my regular stuff, you know? Like, playing pickup basketball on the weekends or just the physical work at the shop. I don't go out running for hours or hit the gym until I drop or anything like that. I just try to stay active enough to keep my energy up. That's pretty standard for someone trying to stay healthy, right? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right idea. I'm definitely not overdoing it.\n\nDoctor: Would you say your weight or shape has a strong influence on how you see yourself or how you feel about your overall worth?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I wouldn't say it's my whole worth or anything. I mean, I hope I don't come across as vain. \n\nI just... I like to look presentable, you know? Like I've got my life together. I think anyone would feel a little down if they let themselves go, right? But it's not like I'm staring in the mirror all day or letting it ruin my life. I just take pride in staying sharp and being able to do my job without getting winded. \n\nI think that's a pretty normal way to feel, isn't it? I just want to make sure I'm focusing on the right things. I definitely don't think my whole value as a person is just about my weight.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your weight or body shape has been affecting how you feel about yourself more than it does for most people?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I really don't think so. I mean, I hope I'm not giving you the wrong impression here. \n\nI think I'm just like any other guy my age trying not to get a beer belly, you know? Most of the guys at the shop, they joke about getting older and putting on weight, and nobody wants that. So I just try to keep it in check. I don't think I care about it more than the next guy, I just... maybe I'm just a little better at sticking to my guns about it? \n\nI just want to be healthy and do my job well. That's not too unusual, is it? I feel like most people care about how they look and feel, right? I just want to make sure I'm not making a big deal out of nothing.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that how you feel about yourself goes up and down depending on how you feel about your weight or body shape?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess maybe a little? But not in a crazy way, you know? I hope I'm explaining this right. \n\nI mean, if I step on the scale and I've kept things where I want them, sure, I feel pretty good about myself. It feels like I'm doing something right, like I'm staying disciplined. And if I feel a little heavy or sluggish, maybe I get a little frustrated with myself. But I don't let it ruin my whole day or anything. \n\nI think that's pretty normal, right? Like, anyone feels better when they're hitting their goals. I just like to know I'm staying on track. That doesn't sound too extreme, does it? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right picture.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever made yourself vomit, used laxatives, or taken any kind of pills or supplements specifically to control your weight?\n\nPatient: Oh, wow. Um... no. No, definitely not. I hope I haven't made it sound like I'm doing anything... you know, dangerous or extreme. \n\nI would never make myself sick or take any of those laxatives or weird diet pills. That just seems really unhealthy, right? I'm just trying to take care of myself and stay in shape for work. I just stick to being disciplined about what I eat. \n\nI know you probably have to ask everyone that, but I promise I'm going about it the right way. That's a good thing, isn't it? I really wouldn't want you to think I'm doing anything crazy like that.\n\nDoctor: Has your eating habits or weight loss affected your ability to function normally in areas like work, social activities, or family life?\n\nPatient: Well, um... for the most part, no, definitely not. I'm still keeping up with everything at the garage just fine, and I'm still playing in my basketball league, so I've got plenty of energy. I hope that shows I'm doing okay, right? \n\nI guess... well, if I'm being completely honest, maybe there's a little bit of tension at home? But it's nothing major, I promise. It's just that I kind of avoid sitting down for the regular family meals... they can just be a bit much, you know, with all the heavy food? So people keep asking if I'm okay, and they worry a little. \n\nBut I'm still functioning perfectly fine everywhere else. It's not a huge deal to just want to eat on my own schedule, is it? I really hope you don't think it's becoming a problem. I'm just trying to stay disciplined.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like your weight or body shape controls how you feel about yourself, even if others don't see it the same way?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess I wouldn't say it *controls* how I feel. I mean, sure, I like to look a certain way, especially since I'm trying to stay fit for basketball and work. But it's not like it's the only thing I care about, you know? \n\nI know my wife sometimes says I'm too hard on myself when I look in the mirror, but I just think I have high standards. That's a good trait to have, isn't it? Being disciplined? I don't think it completely takes over my life or anything. I just like to feel good about the effort I'm putting in. I hope that makes sense, and I'm not making it sound worse than it is.\n\nDoctor: Do you see yourself as overweight or heavier than you actually are, even when others might tell you that you're at a healthy or even low weight?\n\nPatient: Oh, no. No, I definitely don't think I'm overweight. I mean... I know I've leaned out. I know exactly what I look like. \n\nMy wife, she sometimes says I'm getting too thin, but... I don't look in the mirror and see a heavy guy or anything like that. I just see, you know, the spots where I could still tighten up a little bit. Just trying to keep the core strong for when I'm lifting parts or working under cars at the shop. \n\nI don't have some weird, like, distorted view in my head. I just want to stay on top of it and not get sloppy. That's a pretty normal way for a guy to look at himself, right? I really hope you don't think I'm losing touch with reality or anything. I promise I'm just being realistic about my fitness.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any other difficulties in your daily life, such as trouble concentrating, feeling isolated, or having conflicts with others, because of your eating habits or concerns about your weight?\n\nPatient: Well, um... no, not really. I guess, like I said before, the family meals can be a little tricky, so maybe I spend a bit more time doing my own thing around dinner. But it's not like I'm hiding away from everyone, I promise. I'm still playing basketball with the guys, still hanging out. \n\nAnd conflicts... well, just that little bit of worry from my wife, but we don't really fight about it. She just cares, you know? I hope I'm not giving you the wrong idea.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt distressed or upset because you believe you should weigh less or be more muscular, even if it’s not obvious to others?\n\nPatient: Well, um... ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, I mean... I wouldn't say *overly* influenced. I think any guy wants to feel good about how he looks, especially when you hit your forties, right? You don't want to be the guy with the beer belly at the shop. 

So, yeah, I like feeling fit and knowing I'm keeping the weight off. It makes me feel... accomplished, I guess? Like I have good self-control. But I wouldn't say it's the *only* thing I care about. I still feel good about being a good mechanic and providing for my family. 

I just... I like knowing I'm in shape. It's just a normal part of taking care of yourself, don't you think? I really hope it doesn't sound like I'm, you know, superficial or anything like that. I'm just trying to be a healthy guy.

I just stick to being disciplined about what I eat. \n\nI know you probably have to ask everyone that, but I promise I'm going about it the right way. That's a good thing, isn't it? I really wouldn't want you to think I'm doing anything crazy like that.\n\nDoctor: Has your eating habits or weight loss affected your ability to function normally in areas like work, social activities, or family life?\n\nPatient: Well, um... for the most part, no, definitely not. I'm still keeping up with everything at the garage just fine, and I'm still playing in my basketball league, so I've got plenty of energy. I hope that shows I'm doing okay, right? \n\nI guess... well, if I'm being completely honest, maybe there's a little bit of tension at home? But it's nothing major, I promise. It's just that I kind of avoid sitting down for the regular family meals... they can just be a bit much, you know, with all the heavy food? So people keep asking if I'm okay, and they worry a little. \n\nBut I'm still functioning perfectly fine everywhere else. It's not a huge deal to just want to eat on my own schedule, is it? I really hope you don't think it's becoming a problem. I'm just trying to stay disciplined.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like your weight or body shape controls how you feel about yourself, even if others don't see it the same way?\n\nPatient: Well, um... ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, um... no, I don't think so. I really wouldn't say it's stronger for me than it is for most guys my age. I mean, if you look at the other guys down at the shop, we all kind of talk about it, you know? Trying not to let ourselves go, wanting to stay strong... it's just part of getting older. 

I'd say I'm pretty average in that department. I just want to feel comfortable in my own skin and know that I'm taking care of myself. I don't think I take it any further than... well, than what's considered normal. Everyone wants to feel confident, right? I'm just doing what I can to keep that up. I really hope I'm answering this the right way for you... I just don't think I'm any different from anybody else when it comes to that.

I'm definitely not overdoing it.\n\nDoctor: Would you say your weight or shape has a strong influence on how you see yourself or how you feel about your overall worth?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I just take pride in staying sharp and being able to do my job without getting winded. \n\nI think that's a pretty normal way to feel, isn't it? I just want to make sure I'm focusing on the right things. I definitely don't think my whole value as a person is just about my weight.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your weight or body shape has been affecting how you feel about yourself more than it does for most people?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I really don't think so. I mean, I hope I'm not giving you the wrong impression here. \n\nI think I'm just like any other guy my age trying not to get a beer belly, you know? I just stick to being disciplined about what I eat. \n\nI know you probably have to ask everyone that, but I promise I'm going about it the right way. That's a good thing, isn't it? I really wouldn't want you to think I'm doing anything crazy like that.\n\nDoctor: Has your eating habits or weight loss affected your ability to function normally in areas like work, social activities, or family life?\n\nPatient: Well, um... for the most part, no, definitely not. I'm still keeping up with everything at the garage just fine, and I'm still playing in my basketball league, so I've got plenty of energy. I hope that shows I'm doing okay, right? \n\nI guess... well, if I'm being completely honest, maybe there's a little bit of tension at home? But it's nothing major, I promise. It's just that I kind of avoid sitting down for the regular family meals... they can just be a bit much, you know, with all the heavy food? So people keep asking if I'm okay, and they worry a little. \n\nBut I'm still functioning perfectly fine everywhere else. It's not a huge deal to just want to eat on my own schedule, is it? I really hope you don't think it's becoming a problem. I'm just trying to stay disciplined.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like your weight or body shape controls how you feel about yourself, even if others don't see it the same way?\n\nPatient: Well, um... That's a good trait to have, isn't it? Being disciplined? I don't think it completely takes over my life or anything. I just like to feel good about the effort I'm putting in. I hope that makes sense, and I'm not making it sound worse than it is.\n\nDoctor: Do you see yourself as overweight or heavier than you actually are, even when others might tell you that you're at a healthy or even low weight?\n\nPatient: Oh, no. No, I definitely don't think I'm overweight. I mean... I know I've leaned out. I know exactly what I look like. \n\nMy wife, she sometimes says I'm getting too thin, but... I hope I'm not giving you the wrong idea.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt distressed or upset because you believe you should weigh less or be more muscular, even if it’s not obvious to others?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I wouldn't use the word *distressed*. That sounds a little extreme, you know? But it doesn't upset me or ruin my day. \n\nIt's more just... a goal, I guess? Like a motivation to keep up with my routine. I don't get worked up about it or anything. I just want to make sure I'm explaining it right, so it doesn't sound like I'm obsessing over it.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that how you feel about yourself is overly influenced by your weight or body shape, even if others might not see it the same way?\n\nPatient: Well, I mean... But it's not like it's the only thing I care about, you know? \n\nI know my wife sometimes says I'm too hard on myself when I look in the mirror, but I just think I have high standards. That's a good trait to have, isn't it? Being disciplined? I don't think it completely takes over my life or anything. I just like to feel good about the effort I'm putting in. I hope that makes sense, and I'm not making it sound worse than it is.\n\nDoctor: Do you see yourself as overweight or heavier than you actually are, even when others might tell you that you're at a healthy or even low weight?\n\nPatient: Oh, no. No, I definitely don't think I'm overweight. I mean... I know I've leaned out. I know exactly what I look like. \n\nMy wife, she sometimes says I'm getting too thin, but... I don't look in the mirror and see a heavy guy or anything like that. I just see, you know, the spots where I could still tighten up a little bit. Just trying to keep the core strong for when I'm lifting parts or working under cars at the shop. \n\nI don't have some weird, like, distorted view in my head. I just want to stay on top of it and not get sloppy. That's a pretty normal way for a guy to look at himself, right? I really hope you don't think I'm losing touch with reality or anything. I promise I'm just being realistic about my fitness.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any other difficulties in your daily life, such as trouble concentrating, feeling isolated, or having conflicts with others, because of your eating habits or concerns about your weight?\n\nPatient: Well, um... no, not really.
